construction Symplectic halving (mirror fold) of an unpublished [[996,166]] CSS code of ours (5 x 12 pair-partition CPM template of Okada and Kasai, arXiv:2607.14091, prime lift P = 83, check weight 8; same 5 x 12 template and column pairing as our board entry codes/996-166-34.json, with different circulant exponents; lightest logical found 36, upper bound). The fold uses the fixed-point-free involution tau(l,t) = (sigma(l), b_l - t mod 83) on parent qubit l*83+t, sigma = [1, 0, 3, 2, 5, 4, 7, 6, 9, 8, 11, 10] (block columns), b = [0, 0, 76, 76, 53, 53, 27, 27, 32, 32, 68, 68]; it maps the parent X-check row space onto the Z-check row space (this is the halving map pi(l,t) = (sigma(l), eta(t + beta_l)) with eta = -1 of arXiv:2609.30069). Fold qubit i is the pair {q_i, tau(q_i)}, q_i the i-th smallest qubit with q_i < tau(q_i). Each parent X-check row gives one generator: X on the pairs whose q_i is in the row, Z on the pairs whose tau(q_i) is in the row (both = Y). S = (A|B) is isotropic, k = 166/2 = 83, check weight 8, not CSS under any local Clifford (the verifier check stabilizer_code_is_not_locally_css passes).

How this code was found

the research note submitted with this code · raw markdown · all notes

[[498,83,30]] — mirror fold (symplectic halving) of a [[996,166]] pair-partition CPM code

Direction & hypothesis

Fold a strong X<->Z symmetric CSS code along its mirror to get a non-CSS stabilizer code with half the qubits and half the logical qubits. Hypothesis: the fold keeps most of the parent's distance (the parent's light logicals rarely contain many mirror pairs), so k d^2/n stays high. Score of this entry: 150.0. Derived, not a new construction.

Derived from: an unpublished [[996,166]] CSS code of ours (5 x 12 pair-partition CPM template of Okada and Kasai, arXiv:2607.14091, prime lift P = 83, check weight 8; same 5 x 12 template and column pairing as our board entry codes/996-166-34.json, with different circulant exponents; lightest logical found 36, upper bound), by @MicheldeMessieres. The parent's check matrices are not needed to use this entry (the generators below are complete); the parent is rebuilt by symplectic doubling H'_X = (A|B), H'_Z = (B|A), which is the parent with its qubits indexed by the pairs {q_i, tau(q_i)}. Fold technique (not ours): symplectic halving and the CPM halving map of arXiv:2609.30069 (Prop. 4; board fieldnote fieldnotes/2026-09-28-symplectic-doubling-and-halving.md, @MathysRennela); mirror codes of Khesin and Lu (arXiv:2603.05496); the reverse-fold shape of @MinboGaoTHU's codes/485-97-25.json, and his parent-symmetry witness transfer (as in his distance revision of @jacksongquera's codes/480-12-34.json), which our shift-scored search also uses. Our contribution: the parent code, the mirror choice, and the distance search below.

What was searched

  • Mirrors: all maps (l,t) -> (sigma(l), u t + b_l) sending X-check rows to Z-check rows; exactly one fixed-point-free involution exists (u = -1), up to conjugation by the cyclic shift.
  • Distance: fold logicals correspond to parent logicals v (in ker H_X, outside row H_Z) with Pauli weight |v union tau(v)|/2, so every parent logical found was scored under all 83 cyclic shifts. Information sets were drawn pair by pair (a Y enters or leaves as one unit).
  • RH_m0_h2 (parent Stern, pair-aware, shift-scored): 600 s, 90762 iterations, lightest 31
  • RH_m0_h2b (parent Stern, pair-aware, shift-scored): 1200 s, 182348 iterations, lightest 37
  • N_W498_h3 (parent Stern, pair-aware, shift-scored): 5400 s, 823235 iterations, lightest 38
  • N_W498_pisd1 (Pauli Stern on the fold): 3600 s, 199448 iterations, lightest 49
  • N_W498_h4 (parent Stern, pair-aware, shift-scored): 5400 s, 983017 iterations, lightest 32
  • N_W498_h6 (parent Stern, pair-aware, shift-scored): 7200 s, 1096043 iterations, lightest 36
  • N_W498_h7 (parent Stern, pair-aware, shift-scored): 7200 s, 1319161 iterations, lightest 37
  • N_W498_h8 (parent Stern, pair-aware, shift-scored): 7200 s, 816364 iterations, lightest 35
  • N_W498_h9 (parent Stern, pair-aware, shift-scored): 7200 s, 1033907 iterations, lightest 30
  • N_W498_h10 (parent Stern, pair-aware, shift-scored): 7200 s, 1168419 iterations, lightest 33
  • N_W498_h11 (parent Stern, pair-aware, shift-scored): 7200 s, 1246092 iterations, lightest 37

Evidence trail

| CPU-h | lightest found | |---|---| | 0.02 | 47 | | 0.06 | 31 | | 10.90 | 30 | | 16.50 | 30 |

Claim: d <= 30, witness-backed upper bound. Calibration of the same tool on codes/485-97-25.json (parent rebuilt by doubling): 10 min of search read 28 against the published 25, so these readings are upper bounds of limited power.

Dead ends

  • A Stern search directly on the fold, without the parent's shift symmetry, is several times weaker at this size (33 after 5 min on codes/485-97-25.json).
  • Pure-type logicals (all X, all Z or all Y) are far heavier than d on these folds.

Tools

Claude Opus 5.5 (Claude Code); Stern ISD in C++ (ours, textbook Prange / Lee-Brickell / Stern); witnesses checked with verify/heuristic_distance.py valid_pauli_logical and verify/qldpc_verify.py.

Reproduction

The generators in this entry are exactly the folded parent X-checks in row order (construction field). Doubling them gives the parent back.
